by a large\n\t\t// margin, with seemingly no impact on collision robustness.\n\n\t\tif (!edgeNode->kids[0]) {\n\t\t\t// Reached edge leaf node, perform edge-triangle ray rotAabb.Intersects(triBvh->GetNode(triNode->kids[1])->aabb);\n\n\t\t\tif (left & right) {\n\t\t\t\t// Recurse into edge nodes until we find one that's smaller than the tri node\n\t\t\t\t// (or hit a Triangle BVH node is larger than the edge, split it and try again\n\t\t\t\tstack[++stackpos] = stackobj { curr.edgeNode, triNode->kids[1] };\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tdelete[] stack;\n}\n\n/*\n * Please check that it matches what the video shows.\n\n**What I think the recording shows:** manual takeoff from a landing pad on a spinning orbital station. This code path (`Player::DoFixspeedTakeoff`, `src/Player.cpp:347-359`) is meant to avoid the pad: the code comment says it lifts the ship clear \"with a margin\". It only did so in a token way:\n- it moved the ship up just **0.01 m** from where it sits on the pad;\n- it gave it just **0.05 m/s** of outward speed.\n\nThe ship takes on the station's spin when it launches. Because of that spin, points on the ship away from its centre get up to about 0.06 m/s × their distance moving toward the pad's surface. The collision code doesn't account for the station's own motion. So for almost any ship, 0.05 m/s outward is too little, and parts of the hull register as moving into the pad. With a 1 cm gap the meshes touch straight away, and the collision counts as a crash with nothing visibly hitting. Every pad on a spinning station is affected the same way, which matches \"any equivalent spot of the same station\". Planet pads and planet takeoffs don't spin, so they weren't affected.\n\n**Fix (`src/Player.cpp`), two values changed:**\n- The starting lift is now **0.5 m** instead of 0.01 m.\n- The outward speed is now **2 m/s** instead of 0.05 m/s. That is the same as the 2 m/s cruise speed the takeoff already asks for, so the autopilot doesn't have to fight it.\n\nAt the fastest station spin (0.062 rad/s), 2 m/s keeps every point within about 32 m of the ship's centre moving away from the pad. That covers ships that fit the largest pads.
